\displaystyle\lambda\left(\bar{\gamma}-\gamma\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\gamma}\right)+\lambda\left(\gamma-\underline{\gamma}\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{c}}\right),

(73)

where
\gamma
satisfies

\displaystyle\underline{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma\leqslant

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma},

(74)

and
\bar{\gamma}
and
\underline{\gamma}
are respectively defined in (
71
) and (
72
); the set
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma}
is defined in (
68
); and the measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
are both in
\triangle\left(\mathcal{M}\times\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right)
.

Proof:

The proof is presented in Appendix
D
.
∎

The interest of Theorem
9
is beyond the tightness of the bounds in (
73
), which can be optimized with respect to
\gamma
,
\lambda
, and
Q
. The key observation is the central role played by the hypothesis test in (
V-A
) in the analysis of the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
).
In particular, note that the constants
\bar{\gamma}
in (
71
) and
\underline{\gamma}
in (
72
) are independent of the algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
under study.
The dependence on the algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
and the probability measure on the datasets
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
is via the probabilities
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\gamma}\right)
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\gamma}\right)
, which depend on
\gamma
,
\lambda
, and
Q
; and inform on the probability of choosing the null hypothesis in (
V-A
) under two different assumptions. The former is under the assumption that models are drawn from the specific instance of the algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and the training dataset
\boldsymbol{z}
is drawn from
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
. The latter is under the assumptions that models are drawn from
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
(barycenter of the algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
) and the training dataset
\boldsymbol{z}
is drawn from
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
.

V-B

Connections to Information Measures

The following theorem shows an expression for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) in terms of the sum of the mutual information
I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
and the lautum information
L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
.

Theorem 10

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) and assume that for all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) satisfy

Q\ll P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\ll P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\ll Q
,
with
Q
the
\sigma
-finite measure (
33
).
Then,

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})=\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right),

(75)

where the probability measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
is defined in (
33
); and
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q}
in (
32
).

Proof:

The proof follows from Lemma
4
, which holds under the current assumptions. More specifically, the proof is divided into two parts.
The first part focuses on the difference
\displaystyle\int\bigg(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|Q\right)-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)
in (
34
), which satisfies the following equalities:

\displaystyle\int\Big(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|Q\right)-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)\Big)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

(78)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|Q\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|Q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle-\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

\displaystyle-\int\left(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}Q}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

(79)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\Bigg(\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

(80)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right),

(81)

where the equality in (
80
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 4]
.
Plugging (
81
) into (
34
) leads to

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})=\lambda I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\int\left(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right).

(82)

The second part of the proof focuses on the term
\int D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)
in (
82
). Hence, the following holds

\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(83)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(84)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle+\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right),

(86)

where the equality in (
84
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 3]
;
and the equality in (
85
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 4]
and
[
9
, Lemma
3
]
.
Finally, plugging (
86
) into (
82
) completes the proof.
∎In Theorem
10
, the term

\displaystyle\lambda\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right),

(87)

is of the same form as the one in the right-hand side of (
53
).
Thus, it can also be interpreted in the context of a hypothesis test.
Consider the training dataset
\boldsymbol{z}
in (
4
), which is obtained by sampling from the measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
. Assume that both the algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
and the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
are trained upon the same dataset
\boldsymbol{z}
.
Hence, the hypothesis test consists in determining whether a model
\boldsymbol{\theta}
has been obtained from the probability measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
, which represents a Gibbs algorithm trained upon
\boldsymbol{z}
(
H_{0}
null hypothesis); or from the probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
, which represents the algorithm under study trained upon
\boldsymbol{z}
(
H_{1}
alternative hypothesis). That is,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H_{0}:&&\boldsymbol{\theta}\sim P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\\
H_{1}:&&\boldsymbol{\theta}\sim P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}.\end{array}\right.

The main objective of such a hypothesis test is to determine whether models are being sampled from a Gibbs algorithm or the algorithm under study, assuming that both algorithms have been trained upon the same dataset.
The resulting log-likelihood ratio to decide on either hypothesis in (
V-B
), based on the observation of the pair
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)
, is
\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
.
Hence, given a nonnegative real
\gamma
, the null hypothesis is accepted if
\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\geqslant\gamma
. Conversely, the alternative hypothesis is accepted if
\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)<\gamma
.
The acceptance region of the null hypothesis is the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\in\mathcal{M}\times\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}:\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\geqslant\gamma\right\}.

(91)

Consider the following probabilities

\displaystyle P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{c}}\right),\mbox{ and }

(92)

\displaystyle P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{c}}\right).

(93)

The former represents the probability of choosing the null hypothesis under the assumption that the observation
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)
is sampled from the joint probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
.
The latter represents the probability of choosing the null hypothesis under the assumption that the observation
\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)
is sampled from the product probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
.
Consider also the following values

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sup\left\{\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\mathds{R}:\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\in\mathcal{M}\times\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right\}

(94)

and

\displaystyle\underline{\gamma}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\mathds{R}:\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\in\mathcal{M}\times\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right\}.

(95)

Using this notation, the following theorem strengthens the connexion between the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) and the hypothesis test in (
V-B
).

Theorem 11

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) and assume that for all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, the measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
,
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
), and the
\sigma
-finite measure
Q
in (
33
), satisfy
Q\ll P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\ll P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\ll Q
.
Then,

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\underline{\gamma}-\gamma\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{c}}\right)+\lambda\left(\gamma-\bar{\gamma}\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)-\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)\leqslant

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\bar{\gamma}-\gamma\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}\right)+\lambda\left(\gamma-\underline{\gamma}\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}^{\textsf{c}}\right),

(96)

where
\gamma
satisfies

\displaystyle\underline{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma\leqslant

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma},

(97)

and
\bar{\gamma}
and
\underline{\gamma}
are respectively defined in (
94
) and (
95
); the set
\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}
is defined in (
91
); and the measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
are both in
\triangle\left(\mathcal{M}\times\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right)
.

Proof:

The proof is presented in Appendix
E
.
∎The lower and upper bounds in Theorem
11
are tight in at least two cases. First, consider that the algorithm under study is the Gibbs algorithm, i.e.,
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
33
). In such a case,
\bar{\gamma}=\underline{\gamma}=0
and thus,
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)=\lambda\left(I\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)
. This case is studied in more detail in Section
V-D
.
The second case is for an algorithm whose output is independent of the training dataset, i.e., an algorithm such that for all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
,
D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)=0
. In this case, the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) is zero. Moreover,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

(98)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

(99)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle-\iint\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right).

(100)

Beyond the tightness of these bounds, which might be optimized by strategically choosing the value of
\gamma
in the case of any arbitrary algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
, the interest of Theorem
11
lies on the following observation. The generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
75
) is the sum of two quantities:
(a)
A first quantity that characterizes the dependence of the models on the training data via the mutual and lautum information, i.e.,
\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)
; and
(b)
a second quantity that characterizes a statistical distance between the algorithm under study and a Gibbs algorithm via the expression in (
87
).
While the first quantity
(a)
is always nonnegative, the second quantity
(b)
might be negative, zero, or positive. As shown in (
100
), it is zero when the algorithm under study is the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
.

Clearly, a variation in any of these quantities,
(a)
or
(b)
, implies a variation in the other, and thus, cannot be separately optimized.
Nonetheless, these quantities place Gibbs algorithms as natural references to evaluate the generalization capabilities of supervised machine learning algorithms.
For instance, the deviation of the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) from the first quantity
\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)
is upper and lower bounded by a function of the smallest and the largest log-likelihoods in the hypothesis test problem in (
V-B
).
More specifically, Theorem
11
, using
\gamma^{\star}\triangleq\frac{\bar{\gamma}+\underline{\gamma}}{2}
, with
\bar{\gamma}
in (
94
) and
\underline{\gamma}
in (
95
), leads to

\displaystyle-\lambda\left(\frac{\bar{\gamma}-\underline{\gamma}}{2}\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma^{\star}}^{\textsf{c}}\right)-\lambda\left(\frac{\bar{\gamma}-\underline{\gamma}}{2}\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma^{\star}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)-\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)\leqslant

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\frac{\bar{\gamma}-\underline{\gamma}}{2}\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma^{\star}}\right)+\lambda\left(\frac{\bar{\gamma}-\underline{\gamma}}{2}\right)P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma^{\star}}^{\textsf{c}}\right).

(101)

This implies that the largest deviation of the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) from the quantity
\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)
is at most
\lambda\left(\frac{\bar{\gamma}-\underline{\gamma}}{2}\right)
, which is the difference between the lower and upper bounds in (
101
).
This leads to the conclusion that the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) can be
well
approximated by
\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)
, when the algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
is
close enough
to the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
.
More specifically, if the absolute value of the statistical distance in (
87
) is smaller than or equal to some
\delta\geqslant 0
, that is,

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(1-\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{z}\right)\right|

(102)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{\lambda},

then,

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)-\lambda\left(I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right)\right|\leqslant\delta,

(103)

which justifies such an approximation.

V-C

Connections to Euclidean Geometry

Another expression for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
), which leads to interesting connections to Euclidean geometry is presented hereunder.

Theorem 12

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) and assume that for all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
,
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\ll Q
, with
Q
the
\sigma
-finite measure (
33
). Then,

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\iint\bigg(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)

(104)

\displaystyle-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right),

where the probability measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
satisfies (
33
); and
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q}
in (
32
).

Proof:

The proof consists in re-writing the difference
\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)
in (
10
) in terms of an algorithm-driven gap, i.e., using the functional
\mathsf{G}
in (
20
). More specifically, consider the equalities

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{u},P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}},P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)+\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)

(105)

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{z},P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}},P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)+\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right),

(106)

that implies,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

(107)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{u},P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}},P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)+\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{z},P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}},P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)+\lambda D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\|Q\right)

\displaystyle-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)-\lambda D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|Q\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\bigg(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\bigg)

(108)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(Q\right)-\lambda D\left(Q\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(Q\right)+\lambda D\left(Q\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\bigg(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\bigg),

(109)

where the equality in (
108
) follows from
[
56
, Theorem
1
]
, which holds under the assumption of the theorem and
[
9
, Lemma
3
]
;
and the equality in (
109
) follows from
[
9
, Lemma
20
]
.
Finally, from (
109
) and Definition
4
, it follows that

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

(110)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\left(\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\left(\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{u}}\left(Q\right)-\lambda D\left(Q\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle-\iint\left(\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(Q\right)-\lambda D\left(Q\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda\iint\bigg(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)

\displaystyle-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(111)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\iint\bigg(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\right)

(112)

\displaystyle-D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right),

(113)

which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem
12
expresses the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) as the difference between the expectations of two relative entropies. The first relative entropy compares the algorithm
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
to the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
, under the assumption that such algorithms are trained on independent datasets drawn from the same probability distribution
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
. The second relative entropy compares these algorithms under the assumption that they are trained on the same dataset drawn from
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
.
In particular, the first relative entropy satisfies the following property.

Lemma 13

Consider a probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\in\triangle\left(\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right)
and a conditional probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{M}|\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right)
and assume that for all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
,

•

The measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) are absolutely continuous with respect to the
\sigma
-finite measure
Q
in (
33
);

•

The measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
is absolutely continuous with respect to
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
.

Then,

\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(114)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\left(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)+D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right),

where the probability measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
satisfies (
33
); and
\lambda\in\mathcal{K}_{Q}
, with
\mathcal{K}_{Q}
in (
32
).

Proof:

The proof follows by noticing that

\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(115)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle+\iint\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle+\iint\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\left(D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)+D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right),

(116)

which completes the proof.
∎

The converse of the Pythagorean theorem
[
76
, Book I, Proposition
48
]
together with Lemma
13
, lead to the geometric construction shown in Figure
1
.
Such interpretation becomes more interesting by noticing that, from Theorem
12
, the square of the hypotenuse of the triangle in Figure

satisfies

\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)=\frac{1}{\lambda}\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})

\displaystyle+\int D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right),

(117)

which involves the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (

);
and
the length
\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)}
is the square root of a mutual information, i.e.,
\sqrt{I\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)}
. This leads to the alternative interpretation shown in Figure

.

\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)}~
\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\|P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)}
\sqrt{\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)}
