V-D

Generalization Error of the Gibbs Algorithm

The generalization error of the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
33
), can be immediately obtained from Theorem
8
or Theorem
10
. The following corollary formalizes this observation.

Corollary 14

The generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
), with the measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
33
), is

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\left(I\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)+L\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right).

(118)

\sqrt{L\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)}~
\sqrt{\displaystyle I\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}};P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)}
\sqrt{\frac{1}{\lambda}\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})}

Fig. 3:
Geometric interpretation of Lemma
13
for the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
(Definition
5
).

Corollary
14
has been proved numerous times before using several techniques. For instance, a proof for the case in which
Q
is a probability measure appears in
[
2
]
and
[
3
]
. A proof for the case in which
Q
is a probability measure and datasets are formed by independent and identically distributed data points is presented in
[
57
]
. The more general case in which
Q
is a
\sigma
-finite measure is proved in
[
9
]
.
Interestingly, independently of the choice of
\lambda
and
Q
in (
118
), such an expression does not simplify beyond the product of
\lambda
and the sum of a mutual information and a lautum information.
Figure
3
shows a geometric interpretation of Corollary
14
, which is reminiscent of the Pythagorean theorem mentioned in the previous subsection.

Theorem
12
provides another expression for the generalization error of the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
33
), as shown by the following corollary.

Corollary 15

The generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
), with the measure
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
33
), is

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\displaystyle\int\displaystyle\int D\left(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{u}}\|P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right).

Corollary
15
shows that the generalization error of the Gibbs algorithm
P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
33
) is equal to the average (expectation with respect to
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}~
) of a pair-wise comparison via relative entropy of all the instances of such an algorithm.
The expression in (
) appears to be the simplest form in which the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P^{\left(Q,\lambda\right)}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
can be written. As in the previous case, Corollary
14
, the choice of
\lambda
and
Q
do not appear to lead to further simplification.

VI
Explicit Expressions Obtained Via the Method of Data-Driven Gaps

This section focuses on constructing novel explicit expressions for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
) by exploring different choices of the parameters
P_{S}
and
\beta
in (
52
).
It is important to highlight that in these expressions, the central building-block is the conditional WCDG probability measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}|\mathcal{M}\right)
in (
51
) (Definition
6
).
As in Section V, the identities derived in this section are not proposed as direct computational tools for evaluating the generalization error. Instead, they provide an exact data-driven representation of the generalization error by using the WCDG probability measure as a reference object. Thus, the contribution of the data-driven method of gaps is conceptual: it reveals the information-theoretic structure of the generalization error through comparisons with the WCDG probability measure.

The first three subsections provide explicit expressions for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
aiming at establishing connections to existing results in statistics (statistical hypothesis testing), information theory (information measures), and Euclidean geometry (Pythagorean Theorems), respectively.

VI-A

Connections to Hypothesis Testing

A first connection to statistical hypothesis testing is made by the following theorem.

Theorem 16

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
) and assume that:

(a)

The probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
satisfies (
22
) for some
P_{Z}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
;

(b)

For all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) are mutually absolutely continuous;

(c)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
35
) are mutually absolutely continuous; and

(d)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{Z}
satisfy (
40
) and are both mutually absolutely continuous with the probability measure
P_{S}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
in (
51
).

Then,

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})

(120)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta\Bigg(\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\Bigg),

where the probability measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}
is the WCDG probability measure in (
51
); and
\beta\in\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
, with
\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
in (
50
).

Proof:

Under Assumptions
(a)
,
(b)
,
(c)
, and
(d)
, Lemma
7
holds. Then, the proof follows by observing that the differences
D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{S}\right)
and
D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{S}\right)-D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)
in (
52
) respectively satisfy the following equalities:

\displaystyle D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{S}\right)

(121)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\Bigl(\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}\left(z\right)}-\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}\left(z\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(z\right)

(122)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(z\right),

(123)

where the equality in (
122
) follows from the Assumption
(d)
and
[
74
, Theorem 4]
; and the equality in (
123
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 5]
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{S}\right)-D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)

(124)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\Bigl(\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}(z)-\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}(z)}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}\left(z\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}\left(z\right)

(125)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}\left(z\right),

(126)

where the equality in (
125
) follows from Assumption
(d)
and
[
74
, Theorem 4]
; and the equality in (
126
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 5]
.

Plugging both (
122
) and (
125
) into (
52
) yields (
120
), which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem
16
is reminiscent of Theorem
8
, and thus, it admits a similar analysis in terms of mismatched hypothesis tests
[
59
,
75
]
.
Consider for instance a hypothesis test in which the objective is to determine whether an observed data point
z\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
is generated from either the WCDG probability measure given a model
\boldsymbol{\theta}
, i.e., the measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
in (
51
) (
H_{0}
, null hypothesis); or from the reference probability measure
P_{S}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
, regardless of the model
\boldsymbol{\theta}
, (
H_{1}
, alternative hypothesis):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H_{0}:&&z\sim P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\\
H_{1}:&&z\sim P_{S}.\end{array}\right.

As in Section
V-A
, the hypothesis test in (
VI-A
) is said to be mismatched because none of the hypotheses matches the assumptions on the probability distribution of the observations.
More specifically, the hypothesis test in (
VI-A
) is studied in two different settings.
First, the pair
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
is assumed to be sampled from the joint probability measure
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
, with
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) and
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
40
).
Second, the observation
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
is assumed to be sampled from the product measure
P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
.
Regardless of such scenarios, the log-likelihood ratio to decide based upon the pair
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
on the null or alternative hypothesis in (
VI-A
) is
\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)
.
Essentially, given an
\eta\geqslant 0
, if
\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)\geqslant\eta
, then the null hypothesis is accepted.
Alternatively, the null hypothesis is rejected if
\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)<\eta
.
From these assumptions, the acceptance region of the null hypothesis is the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\eta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)\times\mathcal{M}:\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)\geqslant\eta\right\}.

(130)

That is, given an observation
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
, the null hypothesis is accepted if
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}
; or rejected otherwise, i.e.,
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{\textsf{c}}
.
Consider the following probabilities

\displaystyle P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{\textsf{c}}\right),\mbox{ and }

(131)

\displaystyle P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{\textsf{c}}\right).

(132)

The term
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}\right)
represents the probability of choosing
H_{0}
under the assumption that observations
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
are obtained by sampling
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
.
Alternatively, the term
P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}\right)
represents the probability of choosing
H_{0}
under the assumption that observations
\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
are sampled from
P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
.
Consider also the following constants

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sup\left\{\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)\in\mathds{R}:\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)\times\mathcal{M}\right\}.

(133)

and

\displaystyle\underline{\eta}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\log\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{S}}\left(z\right)\in\mathds{R}:\left(z,\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)\times\mathcal{M}\right\}.

(134)

The reference measure
P_{S}
and the WCDG probability measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}
in the hypothesis test in (
VI-A
), are both exogenous to the study of the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
). Nonetheless, a strong connection exists as shown by the following theorem.

Theorem 17

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
) and assume that:

(a)

The probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
satisfies (
22
) for some
P_{Z}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
;

(b)

For all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) are mutually absolutely continuous;

(c)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
35
) are mutually absolutely continuous; and

(d)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{Z}
satisfy (
40
) and are both mutually absolutely continuous with the probability measure
P_{S}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
in (
51
).

Then,

\displaystyle-\beta\left(\bar{\eta}-\eta\right)P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}\right)-\beta\left(\eta-\underline{\eta}\right)P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{\textsf{c}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\leqslant

\displaystyle\beta\left(\eta-\underline{\eta}\right)P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}^{\textsf{c}}\right)+\beta\left(\bar{\eta}-\eta\right)P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\eta}\right),

(135)

where
\eta
satisfies

\displaystyle\underline{\eta}

\displaystyle\leqslant\eta\leqslant

\displaystyle\bar{\eta},

(136)

and
\bar{\eta}
and
\underline{\eta}
are respectively defined in (
133
) and (
134
); the set
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}
is defined in (
130
); and the measures
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
and
P_{Z}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
are both in
\triangle\left(\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)\times\mathcal{M}\right)
.

Proof:

The proof follows the same steps as those in the proof of Theorem
9
.
∎The upper and lower bounds in Theorem
17
can be optimized by strategically choosing the parameter
\beta
and
P_{S}
of the conditional Gibbs probability measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}|\mathcal{M}\right)
; and the nonnegative real
\eta
.
Nonetheless, the interest of Theorem
17
lies in the fact that it unveils a connection between the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
) and the hypothesis test in (
VI-A
), in which both hypotheses are determined by the WCDG probability measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}
and its corresponding reference measure
P_{S}
.

VI-B

Connections to Information Measures

The following theorem is reminiscent of Theorem
10
, which provides an expression for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
) involving the sum of a mutual and a lautum information between models and data points.

Theorem 18

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
) and assume that:

(a)

The probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
satisfies (
22
) for some
P_{Z}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
;

(b)

For all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) are mutually absolutely continuous;

(c)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
in (
35
) are mutually absolutely continuous; and

(d)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{Z}
satisfy (
40
) and are both mutually absolutely continuous with the probability measure
P_{S}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
in (
51
).

Then,

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})=-\beta\left(I\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}};P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)+L\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}};P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\beta\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\beta\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}\left(z\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

(137)

where the probability measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}~
is defined in (
51
);
\beta\in\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
, with
\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
in (
50
).

Proof:

The proof follows from Lemma
7
, which holds under Assumptions
(a)
,
(b)
,
(c)
, and
(d)
. More specifically, the proof is divided into two parts. The first part focuses on the difference
\int\left(D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{S}\right)-D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{S}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
in (
52
). Following similar steps as in the proof of Theorem
10
, it can be verified that

\displaystyle\int\left(D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{S}\right)-D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{S}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(138)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}};P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right).

Using (
138
) in (
52
) yields

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})

(139)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta I\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}};P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)+\beta\int D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle-\beta\int D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right).

The second part of the proof focuses on the term
\int D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)
in (
139
), which satisfies

\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z}\|P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(140)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}(z)\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\left(z\right)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\Bigg(\int\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)

\displaystyle+\int\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}\left(z\right)\Bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(141)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}};P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)

\displaystyle+\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}\left(z\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

(142)

where the equality in (
140
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 3]
; the equality in (
141
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 4]
and Lemma
38
(in Appendix
A
).
The proof ends by using the equality (
142
) in (
139
), which yields (
137
).
∎

In Theorem
18
, the term

\displaystyle-\beta\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(z\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+\beta\iint\log{\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(z)\right)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z}\left(z\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

(143)

admits a similar analysis as the term in (
87
) in the context of a hypothesis test. In this case, the hypothesis test consists in determining whether a data point
z
has been drawn from the WCDG probability distribution
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}
or from the likelihood of such a data point
z
given the observation of the model
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. This can be formulated as follows

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}H_{0}:&&z\sim P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}\\
H_{1}:&&z\sim P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}},\end{array}\right.

and leads to conclusions similar to those in Section
V-B
.

VI-C

Connections to Euclidean Geometry

The following lemma presents an alternative expression for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
), in terms of the variation of the functional
\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}~
in (
8
) due to changes in the parameter
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
.

Lemma 19

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
) and assume that for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}

(a)

The probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) and the probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
satisfy (
35
);

(b)

for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
are mutually absolutely continuous; and

(c)

For all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) are mutually absolutely continuous.

Then,

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})

(147)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\bigg(\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

where the functional
\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}~
is defined in (
8
).

Proof:

The proof follows from observing two series of equalities. The first equalities are:

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(148)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\left(\int\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{z})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\left(\int\mathsf{L}(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{z})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(149)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{z})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(150)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}}(\boldsymbol{z})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(151)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\left(\int\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}}(\boldsymbol{z})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

(152)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z}),

(153)

where the equality in (
148
) follows from (
8
);
the equality in (
149
) follows by exchanging the order of the integrals
[
63
, Theorem
2.6.6
]
;
the equality in (
150
) follows from (
9
);
the equality in (
151
) follows from Assumption
(b)
and
[
74
, Theorem 2]
;
the equality in (
152
) follows from
[
63
, Theorem
2.6.3
]
; and
the equality in (
153
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem 10]
.
The second equalities are:

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(154)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{L}\left(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(155)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{L}\left(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(156)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{L}\left(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(157)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{L}\left(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{L}\left(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(159)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z}),

(160)

where the equality in (
155
) follows from (
8
);
the equality in (
156
) follows from Assumption
(b)
and
[
74
, Theorem 2]
;
the equality in (
157
) follows from
[
63
, Theorem
2.6.6
]
;
the equality in (
) follows from Assumption
(c)
and
[
74
, Theorem 2]
;
the equality in (
159
) follows from Assumptions
(b)
and
(c)
,
[
74
, Theorem 5]
; and
[
74
, Theorem 11]
;
and the equality in (
160
) follows from (
9
).

From (
153
) and (
160
), it follows that

\displaystyle\iint\bigg(\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)

(161)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})-\int\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{z}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}(\boldsymbol{z})

(162)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{z},P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}\right)

(163)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right),

(164)

where the equality in (
163
) follows from (
20
); and the equality in (
164
) follows from Lemma
3
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
19
introduces an alternative expression for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
that is reminiscent of the expression in (
11
).
More specifically, Lemma
19
presents
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
as the expectation of the variation

\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right),

under the assumption that models
\boldsymbol{\nu}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}
are independently sampled from the same probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
in (
28
).
The following theorem leverages this observation to obtain an equivalent expression for the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
that is of a similar form to the one introduced by Theorem
12
.

Theorem 20

Consider the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}})
in (
11
) and assume that:

(a)

The probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
satisfies (
22
) for some
P_{Z}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
;

(b)

The probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) and the probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
satisfy (
35
);

(c)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
are mutually absolutely continuous;

(d)

For all
\boldsymbol{z}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, the probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{z}}
and
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) are mutually absolutely continuous; and

(e)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{Z}
satisfy (
40
) and are both mutually absolutely continuous with the probability measure
P_{S}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
in (
51
).

Then,

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\iint\bigg(D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

(165)

\displaystyle-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}),

where the measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}
is the WCDG probability measure in (
51
); and
\beta\in\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
, with
\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
in (
50
).

Proof:

The proof follows from Lemma
19
, which holds under Assumptions
(b)
,
(c)
, and
(d)
.
More specifically, the proof consists of re-writing the difference
\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)
in (
147
) using the functional
\mathsf{G}
in (
21
).
Under Assumption
(a)
and Lemma
5
, note that for all
\left(\boldsymbol{\nu},\boldsymbol{\mu}\right)\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}(x,y),

(166)

where the conditional probability measure
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
satisfies (
40
).
Hence, the following holds for all
\left(\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

(167)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu},P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}},P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)+\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y),

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

(168)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu},P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}},P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)+\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\mu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y),

where the functional
\mathsf{G}
is defined in (
21
). From Assumption
(e)
and Lemma
31
(in Section
VIII
), it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu},P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}},P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)

(169)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\bigg(D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P_{S}\right)-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)

\displaystyle-D\left(P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|P_{S}\right)\bigg),

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu},P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}},P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

(170)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\bigg(D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P_{S}\right)-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

\displaystyle-D\left(P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P_{S}\right)\bigg).

Using these equalities, it follows from (
167
) and (
168
) that

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

(171)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{\nu},P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}},P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)+\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y)

\displaystyle-\mathsf{G}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu},P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}},P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)-\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\mu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\bigg(-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)-D\left(P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|P_{S}\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle-\beta\bigg(-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)-D\left(P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P_{S}\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y)

\displaystyle-\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\mu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y)

(172)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)-\beta D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)

\displaystyle+\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y)-\beta D\left(P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\|P_{S}\right)

\displaystyle-\int\ell\left(x,y,\boldsymbol{\mu}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}(x,y)+\beta D\left(P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P_{S}\right)

(173)

where the equality in (
172
) follows from both (
169
) and (
170
).

Therefore, from Lemma
19
, it follows that

\displaystyle\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

(175)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\nu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)-\mathsf{R}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\iint\bigg(D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)

\displaystyle-D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}),

which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem
20
expresses the generalization error as the difference between the expectations of two relative entropies. The first relative entropy compares the likelihood
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
to the WCDG probability measure
P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, under the observation of the same model
\boldsymbol{\theta}
, which is drawn from
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
). The second relative entropy compares these two conditional probability measures under the case that the observed models are drawn independently from the same probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
. More interestingly, the second relative entropy satisfies the following property.

Lemma 21

Consider a probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\in\triangle\left(\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right)
and a conditional probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{M}|\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}\right)
and assume that:

(a)

The probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
satisfies (
22
) for some
P_{Z}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
;

(b)

The probability measures
P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
in (
28
) and the probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
satisfy (
35
);

(c)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measure
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is absolutely continuous with respect to
P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
; and

(d)

For all
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}
, the probability measures
P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
P_{Z}
satisfy (
40
) and are both absolutely continuous with respect to the probability measure
P_{S}\in\triangle\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)
in (
51
).

Then,

\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\bigg(D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{Z}\right)+D\left(P_{Z}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

where the probability measure
P_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\left(P_{S},\beta\right)}~
is defined in (
51
);
\beta\in\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
, with
\mathcal{J}_{P_{S}}
in (
50
).

Proof:

The proof follows by noticing that

\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(176)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\iint\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(z)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iint\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(z)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

(177)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{Z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iint\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(z)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}),

(178)

where the equality in (
177
) follows from Assumptions
(a)
and
(c)
together with Lemma
37
(in Appendix
A
) and
[
74
, Theorem
2
]
.

The rest of the proof follows from (
178
) by noticing that

\displaystyle\iint\int\log{\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(z)}\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

(179)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\int\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(z)\right)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(z)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\int\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}}{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\right)\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

(180)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iint\log\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}P_{Z}}{\mathrm{d}P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}}(z)\right)\mathrm{d}P_{Z}(z)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})

(181)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right),

(182)

where the equality in (
179
) follows from Assumptions
(a)
and
(c)
together with Lemma
37
(in Appendix
A
) and
[
74
, Theorem
2
]
;
the equality in (
180
) follows by exchanging the order of the integrals
[
63
, Theorem
2.6.6
]
;
the equality in (
181
) follows from
[
74
, Theorem
10
]
.
The proof is completed by plugging (
182
) into (
178
).
∎

The converse of the Pythagorean theorem
[
76
, Book I, Proposition
48
]
together with Lemma
21
, lead to the geometric construction shown in Figure
4
.
Note the duality between Figure
1
and Figure
4
.
From Theorem
20
, it follows that

\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})

(183)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\mu}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})-\frac{1}{\beta}\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right),

which involves the generalization error
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
in (
11
).
This observation leads to the construction in Figure
5
, which exhibits a duality with Figure
2
.

\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)}
\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{Z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}
\sqrt{\displaystyle\iint D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\nu}}\right)\bigg)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}

Geometric interpretation of Lemma
21
involving only relative entropies. Note that
\int D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{Z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=I\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}};P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right)
.

\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)}
\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P_{Z}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})}
\sqrt{\displaystyle\int D\left(P_{Z|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|P^{(P_{S},\beta)}_{\hat{Z}|\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\mathrm{d}P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\frac{1}{\beta}\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G}}}\left(P_{\boldsymbol{\Theta}|\boldsymbol{Z}},P_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)}
